451.29065pt}(y)=J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\qquad\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,.

Therefore, by the convolution theorem we get:

Lemma 5.3
.

The function

f_{n}=2nr\sqrt{\pi}G_{n}*\theta_{r}=2nr\sqrt{\pi}I_{0}(2n^{2}r\|x\|)e^{-n^{2}(\|x\|^{2}+r^{2})}\,,

is an approximate
r
-circle with Fourier transform given by

\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-5.7078pt\hbox{$f_{n}$}\hskip-5.7078pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(y)=\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\qquad\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,.

∎

Combining all results from this paper we get:

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and let
r>0
. Then

(5.6)

\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(C_{r})=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}\mu(y)\,.

∎

Remark 5.5
.

When
\mu=\gamma
is an autocorrelation measure, the above formula calculates the diffraction measure
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-2.58865pt\hbox{$\gamma$}\hskip-2.58865pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(C_{r})
for the circle
C_{r}
in terms of the autocorrelation measure
\gamma
.

6.
Applications

In this section, we provide applications of Theorem
5.4
and highlight some interesting observations that could lead to further applications. Below, we use
\lambda
to denote the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, and sometimes use the notation
\mbox{d}\lambda(x)
instead of
\mbox{d}x
in cases where the integral could be mistaken for a line integral.

We start with some simple examples with measures
\mu
where
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(C_{r})
is known and easily evaluated.

Example 6.1
.

Let
\mu=\delta_{x}
for some
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. Then
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(y)=e^{-2\pi ix\cdot y}
and (
5.6
) becomes

\int_{C_{r}}e^{-2\pi ix\cdot y}\,\mbox{d}\lambda(y)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}e^{-\pi^{2}\|x\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|x\|)=0\,,

where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Example 6.2
.

Let
\mu=\theta_{r^{\prime}}
. Recall from Proposition
3.4
that for any
r^{\prime}>0
, we have that

\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-5.91379pt\hbox{$\theta_{r^{\prime}}$}\hskip-5.91379pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(y)=J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|y\|)\qquad\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,.

Thus, in this case, (
5.6
) gives us:

\int_{C_{r}}J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|x\|)\,\mbox{d}\lambda(x)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{r\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{-\pi^{2}(r^{\prime})^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi rr^{\prime})\,\mbox{d}\theta=0\,.

Remark 6.3
.

More generally, if
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
is such that

\mu

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

is an absolutely continuous measure, then

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}\mu(y)=0\,.

Next, let us look at the cases where
\mu
is a character.

Example 6.4
.

Let
\mu=\lambda
be the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. Then
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}=\delta_{0}
and (
5.6
) tells us that for all
r>0
we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=0\,.

Example 6.5
.

Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
and let
\mu
be the function
y\mapsto e^{-2\pi ix\cdot y}
. Then
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}=\delta_{x}
and (
5.6
) becomes

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-2\pi ix\cdot y}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=\begin{cases}1&\|x\|=r\\
0&\|x\|\neq r\,.\end{cases}

Notice that the above limit is just
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}f_{n}(x)
, where
f_{n}
is the approximate
r
-circle defined in Section
5
.

Next, we consider the case where
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}=\theta_{r^{\prime}}
for some
r^{\prime}>0
. This example is of particular interest because it leads to an orthogonality relation for Bessel functions of the form
J_{0}(2\pi r\|\cdot\|)
.
Applying Corollary
4.4
with
\mu=J_{0}(2\pi r\|\cdot\|)
, we get the following:

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
r,r^{\prime}>0
. If
\left\{f_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is any approximate
r
-circle, then the following identity holds:

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-5.7078pt\hbox{$f_{n}$}\hskip-5.7078pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(y)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=\begin{cases}1&r^{\prime}=r\\
0&r^{\prime}\neq r\,.\end{cases}

In particular, when
f_{n}=2nr\sqrt{\pi}G_{n}*\theta_{r}
we have

(6.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{r\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-z^{2}/4n^{2}}J_{0}(rz)J_{0}(r^{\prime}z)z\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{4r\pi^{2}\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}u^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi ru)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}u)u\,\mbox{d}u

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=\begin{cases}1&r^{\prime}=r\\
0&r^{\prime}\neq r\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

Corollary
4.4
applied to
\mu=J_{0}(2\pi r\|\cdot\|)
gives

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=\begin{cases}1&r^{\prime}=r\\
0&r^{\prime}\neq r\,.\end{cases}\,.

Next, switching to polar coordinates gives

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}y=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\pi^{2}u^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi ru)J_{0}(2\pi r^{\prime}u)u\,\mbox{d}u\,.

The substitution
z=2\pi u
yields the remaining formula.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

The integrals that appear in (
6.1
) can be evaluated using standard tables of integrals (e.g.
[
GR00
, 6.633 (2)]
).

The above examples show that Corollary
4.4
and Theorem
5.4
can lead to interesting integral identities. In the next section, we will focus on pure point measures; however, before doing so, let us state here the following result, which follows immediately from the rotational invariance of the pinwheel diffraction
[
MPS06
]
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\gamma
be the autocorrelation of the pinwheel tiling and let
r>0
. Then, there exists a circle of radius
r
in the pinwheel diffraction if and only if

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}\gamma(y)\neq 0\,.

∎

Remark 6.9
.

The same also holds for the autocorrelation and diffraction of any tiling with statistical circular symmetry
[
Fre08
]
, as any such tiling has rotationally invariant diffraction.

6.1.
Measures with pure point Fourier transform.

Next we consider some examples with pure point diffraction. In this case, the left side of (
5.6
) is the sum of the intensities of the Bragg peaks that lie on the circle
C_{r}
. More precisely, we get the following:

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
r>0
and let
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
be such that

\mu

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

is pure point, i.e.

\mu

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

is concentrated on a countable subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. Let
f_{n}
be any approximate
r
-circle.
Then

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-5.7078pt\hbox{$f_{n}$}\hskip-5.7078pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\r 441.65944pt}(y)\,\mbox{d}\mu(y)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\\
\|x\|=r\end{subarray}}\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt}(\{x\})\,.

In particular, when
f_{n}=2nr\sqrt{\pi}G_{n}*\theta_{r}
we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\mbox{d}\mu(y)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\\
\|x\|=r\end{subarray}}\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(\{x\})\,.

∎

This result leads to an interesting formula for the sum of two squares function from number theory. Let us first recall its definition.

Definition 6.11
.

The
sum of two squares function

r_{2}
is the function
r_{2}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
defined as

r_{2}(n):={\rm card}\{(k,l)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}:k^{2}+l^{2}=n\}\,.

Remark 6.12
.

The sum of two squares function has the following properties:

(a)

r_{2}
is an arithmetic function, meaning that
r_{2}(mn)=r_{2}(m)r_{2}(n)
for all relatively prime
m,n
.

(b)

r_{2}
has the alternate formula

r_{2}(n)=4(d_{1}(n)-d_{3}(n))\,,

where
d_{j}(n)
is the number of divisors of
n
which equal
j\pmod{4}
.

(c)

In some places
r_{2}(n)
is called the
shelling function
, see for example
[
BGJR00
]
,
[
BG03
]
.

By the Poisson summation formula, we have that
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-6.84726pt\hbox{$\delta_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}$}\hskip-6.84726pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{17.94456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}=\delta_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
Therefore, we get the following formula for
r_{2}(n)
in terms of Bessel functions. We only list the result using the approximate
r
-circle from Section
5
, but similar formulas can be deduced for any approximate
r
-circle.

Proposition 6.13
.

The sum of two squares function satisfies the following:

(a)

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

r_{2}(k)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2\pi\sqrt{\pi k}}{n}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}r_{2}(m)e^{-\frac{\pi^{2}m}{n^{2}}}J_{0}(2\pi\sqrt{km})\,.

(b)

For all
r>0
such that
r^{2}\notin\mathbb{N}
we have

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}r_{2}(m)e^{-\frac{\pi^{2}m\textbf{}}{n^{2}}}J_{0}(2\pi r\sqrt{m})=0\,.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.10
, for each
r>0
we have

\delta_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}(C_{r})=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2r\pi\sqrt{\pi}}{n}\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\,\,.

Next, grouping the elements in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
by their norm, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\\
\|y\|^{2}=m\end{subarray}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\\
\|y\|^{2}=m\end{subarray}}e^{-\pi^{2}m/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\sqrt{m})=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}r_{2}(m)e^{-\pi^{2}m/n^{2}}J_{0}(2\pi r\sqrt{m})\,.

Now, if
r^{2}=k\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\delta_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}(C_{r})=r_{2}(k)\,,

while when
r^{2}\notin\mathbb{N}
we have

\delta_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}(C_{r})=0\,.

∎

This can easily be extended to arbitrary lattices in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. Indeed, for a locally finite point set
\Lambda\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
denote by
r_{\Lambda}:[0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}}
the function

r_{\Lambda}(k):={\rm card}\{(s,t)\in\Lambda:s^{2}+t^{2}=k\}=\delta_{\Lambda}(C_{\sqrt{k}})\,.

Then, exactly as in Proposition
6.13
we have:

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
L\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
be a lattice with dual lattice
L^{0}
. Then, for all
k>0
we have

r_{L}(k)=\frac{1}{\det(L)}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{2\pi\sqrt{\pi k}}{n}\sum_{m\in[0,\infty)}r_{L^{0}}(m)e^{-\frac{\pi^{2}m}{n^{2}}}J_{0}(2\pi\sqrt{mk})\,.

∎

Appendix A
A family of approximate
r
-circles

In this appendix we offer an alternate approach to constructing approximate
r
-circles via convolution with the annulus

C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}:=\left\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,:\,r-\frac{1}{n}<\|x\|<r+\frac{1}{n}\right\}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

instead of with
\theta_{r}
.

We provide general conditions on a sequence of functions
g_{n}
that suffice for the convolution
g_{n}*1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}
to be an approximate
r
-circle. The resulting formula for
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(C_{r})
is more complicated than that given in Theorem
5.4
; however, the approach yields a large family of approximate
r
-circles, whereas the approach taken in Section
5
is specific to the convolution of
\theta_{r}
with the Gaussian functions
G_{n}
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
r>0
, and let
\left\{g_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be any sequence in
{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
with the following properties:

(i)

g_{n}\geq 0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
;

(ii)

\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(x)\,\mbox{d}x=1
;

(iii)

there exists some
\varphi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
such that

g_{n}(x)\leq\varphi(x)\qquad\forall\|x\|\geq\frac{1}{n}\,.

Then the sequence

f_{n}:=g_{n}*1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

is an approximate
r
-circle.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.8
, since
g_{n}
is a Schwartz function and
1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}
is compactly supported, we have that
f_{n}=g_{n}*1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Next, we need to show that the sequence
\left\{f_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies conditions (C1) and (C2) of Definition
4.1
. We do will this in three steps. First, let us define:

u_{n}:=(g_{n}\cdot 1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}})*1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}\,,\qquad v_{n}:=f_{n}-u_{n}=(g_{n}\cdot 1_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\backslash B_{\frac{1}{n}}})*1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}\,.

Then
f_{n}=u_{n}+v_{n}
.

Step 1.
We show that there exists a constant
C>0
and some sequence
a_{n}\to 0
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
we have

\displaystyle 0\leq u_{n}(x)\leq C\,,\qquad 0\leq v_{n}(x)\leq a_{n}\,.

Observe that:

u_{n}(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}g_{n}(x-y)1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}(x-y)1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}(y)\,\mbox{d}y=\int_{x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(y)1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}(y)\,\mbox{d}y\,,

where

x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}=\left\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,:\,r-\frac{1}{n}<\|x-y\|<r+\frac{1}{n}\right\}\,.

From this and our assumptions we get

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}u_{n}(x)\leq\int_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(y)\,\mbox{d}y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}C<\infty\qquad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,

where the last inequality uses that
\int_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(x)\,\mbox{d}x
is a convergent sequence, and hence is bounded by some
C>0
.

Next, by assumptions (i) and (iii) we have:

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}v_{n}(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\cdot\mbox{\text{Area}}(C_{r,\frac{1}{n}})=:a_{n}\rightarrow 0\,.

Step 2.
We show that
u_{n}(x)\rightarrow 1_{C_{r}}(x)
for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. To prove this, we split the problem into two cases.

•

Case 1:

\|x\|\neq r
.

If
\|x\|\neq r
, then there exists an
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}\cap(x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}})=\emptyset
for all
n\geq N
.
From this and the observation that
g_{n}1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}
is supported in
\overline{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}
, we get

u_{n}(x)=\int_{x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(y)1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}(y)\,\mbox{d}y=\int_{x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}0\,\mbox{d}y=0\,,

for all
n\geq N
, so
u_{n}(x)\rightarrow 0
.

•

Case 2:

\|x\|=r
.

If
\|x\|=r
, then we have
B_{\frac{1}{n}}\subseteq x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. From this, we see that

u_{n}(x)=\int_{x-C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(y)1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}(y)\,\mbox{d}y=\int_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(y)\,\mbox{d}y\,,

so by (ii), we get
u_{n}(x)\rightarrow 1
.

Moreover, by Step 1, we have that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}v_{n}(x)=0
for all
x
, so it follows that condition (C1) is satisfied.

Step 3.
We prove (C2). Observe that
u_{n}
is compactly supported with

\mbox{\rm supp}(u_{n})\subseteq\mbox{\rm supp}(g_{n}\cdot 1_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}})+\mbox{\rm supp}(1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}})\subseteq\overline{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}+\overline{B_{r+\frac{1}{n}}}\subseteq\overline{B_{r+2}}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Fix some
\psi\in C_{\mathsf{c}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
such that
\psi\geq 1_{\overline{B_{r+2}}}
. Then, using positivity, we have

0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}u_{n}\leq C1_{\overline{B_{r+2}}}\leq C\psi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

and

\displaystyle 0\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}v_{n}\leq\varphi*\psi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\,.

It follows that

0\leq f_{n}=u_{n}+v_{n}\leq C\psi+\varphi*\psi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

Therefore, (C2) is satisfied.
∎

Remark A.2
.

The same proof shows that Lemma
A.1
also holds in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

Next, we would like to use this to derive a result similar to Theorem
5.4
that gives a formula for
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(C_{r})
for all
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. We will need the Fourier transform of the characteristic function
1_{B_{R}}
. It turns out that this can be expressed in terms of the following Bessel function
J_{1}
:

Definition A.3
.

For each
t\geq 0
, define

J_{1}(t):=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}sJ_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s&t>0\\
0&t=0\,.\end{cases}

This function is the
Bessel function of the first kind and order 1
.

The above definition of
J_{1}
is equivalent to the integral formula

\int_{0}^{x}uJ_{0}(u)\mbox{d}u=xJ_{1}(x)\qquad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}\,,

which we will use below.

Remark A.4
.

J_{1}
is usually defined as

J_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{i(\theta-t\sin\theta)}\,\mbox{d}\theta\,.

The equivalence of these definitions is most easily seen from the well-known series representations for
J_{0}
and
J_{1}

[
Wat44
, p. 45]
.

Note that, since
1_{B_{R}}
has compact support, its Fourier transform

1_{B_{R}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

must be a continuous function. We get the following formula:

Lemma A.5
.

For any
R>0
,

1_{B_{R}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

is the continuous function given by

(A.1)

\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-8.77052pt\hbox{$1_{B_{R}}$}\hskip-8.77052pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.44456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(y)=\begin{cases}\frac{R}{\|y\|}J_{1}(2\pi R\|y\|)&y\neq 0\\
\pi R^{2}&y=0\end{cases}\qquad\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,.

Proof.

Let
R>0
and
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. When
y=0
, we have

\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-8.77052pt\hbox{$1_{B_{R}}$}\hskip-8.77052pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{17.44456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(0)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}1_{B_{R}}(x)\,\mbox{d}x=\pi R^{2}\,.

Next consider the case when
y\neq 0
.
Since
1_{B_{R}}
is rotational invariant we get

\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip \displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt}(\|y\|,0)=\int_{B_{R}}e^{-2\pi ix\cdot(\|y\|,0)}\mbox{d}x=\int_{0}^{R}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{-2\pi ir\|y\|\cos(\theta)}r\mbox{d}\theta\mbox{d}r

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{0}^{R}rJ_{0}(2\pi r\|y\|)\mbox{d}r\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u=2\pi r\|y\|}}{{=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=}}\frac{1}{2\pi\|y\|^{2}}\int_{0}^{2\pi R\|y\|}uJ_{0}(u)\mbox{d}u=\frac{R}{\|y\|}J_{1}(2\pi R\|y\|)\,,

as claimed.

∎

This lets us evaluate

1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

using Lemma
A.5
:

Corollary A.6
.

For any
r>0
and
n>\frac{1}{r}
, we have

(A.2)

\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-12.61482pt\hbox{$1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}$}\hskip-12.61482pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{17.44456pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}(y)=\begin{cases}\frac{nr+1}{n\|y\|}J_{1}\left(\frac{2\pi\|y\|(nr+1)}{n}\right)-\frac{nr-1}{n\|y\|}J_{1}\left(\frac{2\pi\|y\|(nr-1)}{n}\right)&y\neq 0\\
\pi\left(r+\frac{1}{n}\right)^{2}-\pi\left(r-\frac{1}{n}\right)^{2}&y=0\end{cases}\qquad\forall y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,.

∎

As shown above, we have an explicit formula for the Fourier transform

1_{C_{r,\frac{1}{n}}}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

. Thus, given a Schwartz function
g_{n}
as in Lemma

, we can use the convolution theorem to get

which would allow us to compute explicitly the Fourier transform

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

provided that

g_{n}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

can also be computed explicitly. In particular, combining Lemma
4.2
, Lemma
A.1
, and Corollary
A.6
gives us the following general theorem:

Theorem A.7
.

Let

. If

satisfying the conditions of Lemma

where
\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt}(y)
is given by (

). ∎

Remark A.8
.

The above formula (
A.3
) also holds when
\left\{g_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of compactly supported functions in
L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\cap L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
satisfying

\mbox{\rm supp}(g_{n})\subseteq\overline{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,,

and conditions (i) and (ii) of Lemma
A.1
, provided that

\mu

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

is translation bounded. This can be seen from
[
RS17a
, Theorem 3.12]
and the Fourier transformability of
\mu
as a measure (see Remark
2.6
).

We conclude with an example using a sequence of Gaussian functions and Theorem
A.7
to produce an alternate formula (
A.5
) for the intensity of a circle.

Example A.9
.

Consider the sequence of Gaussian functions

g_{n}(x):=\frac{n^{4}}{\pi}e^{-n^{4}\|x\|^{2}}\qquad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\ \forall n\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Then
g_{n}\geq 0
and

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\frac{1}{n}}}g_{n}(x)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{4}}{\pi}\int_{\|x\|\leq\frac{1}{n}}e^{-n^{4}\|x\|^{2}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y=n^{2}x}}{{=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=\joinrel=}}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\|y\|\leq n}e^{-\|y\|^{2}}\,\mbox{d}y\rightarrow\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\|y\|^{2}}\,\mbox{d}y=1\,,

so
\{g_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies conditions (i) and (ii) of Lemma
A.1
.

Next, to verify condition (iii), we demonstrate that, for the Schwartz function

\varphi(x):=\frac{4}{\pi}e^{1-\|x\|^{2}}\qquad\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,,

we have
g_{n}(x)\leq\varphi(x)
whenever
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\backslash B_{\frac{1}{n}}
. Equivalently, we want to verify that

(A.4)

\frac{n^{4}}{4}\leq e^{1+(n^{4}-1)t^{2}}\qquad\forall t\geq\frac{1}{n}\,.

Indeed, for
t\geq\frac{1}{n}
, we have

1+(n^{4}-1)t^{2}\geq 1+n^{2}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\geq n^{2}\,,

so

\ln\left(\frac{n^{4}}{4}\right)=2\ln\left(\frac{n^{2}}{2}\right)\leq 2\cdot\frac{n^{2}}{2}=n^{2}\leq 1+(n^{4}-1)t^{2}=\ln\left(e^{1+(n^{4}-1)t^{2}}\right)\,.

This verifies (
A.4
).

Thus, we have shown that
\left\{g_{n}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies the conditions of Lemma
A.1
, so given a measure
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and some
r>0
, we can apply Theorem
A.7
to obtain the following formula for
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-3.01274pt\hbox{$\mu$}\hskip-3.01274pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\r (A.5)

\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-2.58865pt\hbox{$\gamma$}\hskip-2.58865pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{15.30566pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}(C_{r})=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}e^{-\pi^{2}\|y\|^{2}/n^{4}}\\
\times\left[\frac{nr+1}{n\|y\|}J_{1}\left(\frac{2\pi\|y\|(nr+1)}{n}\right)-\frac{nr-1}{n\|y\|}J_{1}\left(\frac{2\pi\|y\|(nr-1)}{n}\right)\right]\,\mbox{d}\gamma(y)\,.

for any
\mu\in\mathcal{S}\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
and any
r>0
.
